			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Introducing the Web Search API on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/introducing-the-web-search-api-on-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Shubham Divekar]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 11 Nov 2026 23:13:16 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Intermediate (200)]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">811b8dfa171613df698db45e0ea693304815500b</guid>

					<description>Today we're announcing the Web Search API on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore: your own code can search the web with a single signed API call, with no gateway and no resources to create. Call it from the AWS SDKs or the AWS CLI and use it in applications, agent loops, and data pipelines.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;AI agents and applications that answer questions about recent events, product releases, prices, or regulations need information newer than their model’s training data. Until now, &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/gateway-target-connector-web-search-tool.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Web Search on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt; delivered that information to agents through &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/gateway.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Gateway&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore. That means creating a gateway and a web search target and calling the tool from an MCP client. The setup fits platform teams that share one search policy across many agents, but it’s more than you need when your own code only requires search results.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Today, we’re announcing the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/web-search-api.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Web Search API&lt;/a&gt; on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore. With it, your own code can search the web with a single API call. It returns ranked results from a web index that Amazon operates, each with a text extract and, when available, the page title, URL, and publication time. You call it from the AWS SDKs or the AWS Command Line Interface (AWS CLI), signed with the same &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/iam/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) credentials&lt;/a&gt; you use for your other AWS services. There are no resources to create first. Authenticate, call, and get results. Use it in an application, an agent loop you write yourself, or a data pipeline.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="why-a-standalone-api"&gt;Why a standalone API?&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/gateway.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Gateway&lt;/a&gt; is the recommended path for most agentic workloads. It gives agents one secure Model Context Protocol (MCP) endpoint that combines web search with your other tools. It also handles inbound and outbound authentication, semantic tool selection, and optional &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/gateway-sessions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;MCP sessions&lt;/a&gt;. But developers told us they also need a lower-level integration point for scenarios where the gateway is not the right fit:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Custom orchestration frameworks –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Developers building their own agent loops, or using frameworks like LangChain or LlamaIndex, want to call web search directly as a tool within their orchestration logic rather than routing through a gateway.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Non-agent use cases –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Not every application that needs web grounding is an agent. Search-powered dashboards, fact-checking pipelines, content enrichment workflows, and monitoring systems all benefit from real-time web results without requiring agent infrastructure.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Tighter control over the request lifecycle –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Some developers want to manage retries, caching, and error handling themselves rather than delegating to the gateway.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Simpler integration for existing applications –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Applications that already have their own API layer can call the Web Search API directly, adding web grounding without adopting the full AgentCore Gateway stack.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Web Search API complements the gateway. It runs on the same search infrastructure that powers Alexa+, Kiro, Quick, and other Amazon products, with the same quality and compliance posture, exposed as a direct API operation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="how-it-works"&gt;How it works&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Web Search API is the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/APIReference/API_InvokeWebSearch.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;InvokeWebSearch&lt;/a&gt; operation on the &lt;code&gt;bedrock-agentcore&lt;/code&gt; data plane, the same endpoint that other AgentCore tools such as &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/browser-tool.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Browser&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, use. Requests are signed with &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/reference_sigv.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Signature Version 4 (SigV4)&lt;/a&gt; from your standard AWS credentials, so there’s no API key or secret to manage.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ML-22165-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ML-22165-1.png" alt="Diagram of a Web Search API request flowing from the caller through IAM authorization and filtering to the Amazon web index and back" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: How a Web Search API request flows&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The numbered steps are:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Your application calls &lt;code&gt;InvokeWebSearch&lt;/code&gt; with the AWS SDKs or the AWS CLI, which sign the request with SigV4 using its AWS credentials. From a virtual private cloud (VPC), the call can go through an interface VPC endpoint.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The API checks with AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) that the caller has &lt;code&gt;bedrock-agentcore:InvokeWebSearch&lt;/code&gt; on the AWS managed web search tool.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The API applies the filters in the request: the domains to include or exclude and the publication time range.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The API searches a web index that Amazon operates. Queries are served inside AWS and aren’t sent to a third-party search engine.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The API returns up to &lt;code&gt;maxResults&lt;/code&gt; ranked results, each with a text extract and, when available, the page title, URL, and publication time.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The calling identity needs one &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/introduction.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;IAM&lt;/a&gt; permission on an AWS managed resource. It’s the same action and resource that a gateway’s service role uses for web search, so one policy covers both routes. For details, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/web-search-api.html#web-search-api-permissions" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Grant permission to call the API&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
    "Version": "2012-10-17",
    "Statement": [
        {
            "Sid": "InvokeWebSearch",
            "Effect": "Allow",
            "Action": "bedrock-agentcore:InvokeWebSearch",
            "Resource": "arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:us-west-2:aws:tool/web-search.v1"
        }
    ]
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="a-first-search-request"&gt;A first search request&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following example searches two trusted domains for pages published in 2026, in the US West (Oregon) Region (us-west-2), with the &lt;a href="https://boto3.amazonaws.com/v1/documentation/api/latest/index.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS SDK for Python (Boto3)&lt;/a&gt;:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;import boto3

client = boto3.client("bedrock-agentcore", region_name="us-west-2")

response = client.invoke_web_search(
    webSearchId="web-search.v1",
    searchQuery="multi-region EKS cluster deployment best practices",
    maxResults=10,
    filters={
        "domainFilter": {"include": ["aws.amazon.com", "kubernetes.io"]},
        "publishedDateFilter": {"publishedAfter": "2026-01-01T00:00:00Z"},
    },
)

for result in response["results"]:
    print(result.get("publishedAt"), result.get("title"), result.get("url"))&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Every result has a text extract. The title, URL, and publication date come back when the index has them, so read them with &lt;code&gt;get&lt;/code&gt;. One result from a live call, shortened:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
    "title": "One Amazon EKS, many edges: How to choose your edge container strategy on AWS",
    "url": "https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/containers/one-amazon-eks-many-edges-how-to-choose-your-edge-container-strategy-on-aws/",
    "text": "Separate the standard from the deployment model Choosing the wrong platform isn't really the issue. ...",
    "publishedAt": "2026-09-25T15:52:00Z"
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;That’s the full integration. If your code runs on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lambda&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ecs/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Elastic Container Service (Amazon ECS)&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ec2/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Elastic Compute Cloud (Amazon EC2)&lt;/a&gt;, or &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/eks/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Elastic Kubernetes Service (Amazon EKS)&lt;/a&gt;, the execution role provides credentials automatically. Add the permission to that role, and the same code runs unchanged.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="key-api-features"&gt;Key API features&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Domain filtering –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Include or exclude specific domains to scope search results to trusted sources (for example, only aws.amazon.com and kubernetes.io for an assistant focused on AWS). Each list holds up to 100 domain names, and a root domain also matches its subdomains.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Date filtering –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Restrict results to a publication time range with &lt;code&gt;publishedAfter&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;publishedBefore&lt;/code&gt;, given as RFC 3339 date-times in UTC such as 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z. Both bounds are inclusive.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Configurable result count –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Request between 1 and 25 results per query. If you don’t set &lt;code&gt;maxResults&lt;/code&gt;, the API returns up to 25. The search text, &lt;code&gt;searchQuery&lt;/code&gt;, can be up to 200 characters.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Results with text extracts –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Each result includes a text extract and, when available, the page title, URL, and publication time, ready to feed into your model’s context window.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="getting-started-in-under-5-minutes"&gt;Getting started in under 5 minutes&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Here are two ways to get a first result:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="option-1-aws-cloudshell-no-local-setup"&gt;Option 1: AWS CloudShell (no local setup)&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Open &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cloudshell/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS CloudShell&lt;/a&gt; in your browser. It uses the credentials of your console session.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Your role will have the &lt;code&gt;bedrock-agentcore:InvokeWebSearch&lt;/code&gt; permission, from the preceding policy or from the broader AWS managed policy &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/aws-managed-policy/latest/reference/BedrockAgentCoreFullAccess.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;BedrockAgentCoreFullAccess&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Run: 
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws bedrock-agentcore invoke-web-search \
    --web-search-id web-search.v1 \
    --search-query "latest EKS features" \
    --max-results 5&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
  &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="option-2-local-development"&gt;Option 2: Local development&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Install the AWS CLI, or the AWS SDK for your language. For Python, use Boto3 1.43.127 or later.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Create an IAM role with the preceding policy.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Get credentials for that role: assume it from your application or an AWS CLI profile, or sign in with it through your identity provider.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Run the preceding Python example, or call the API from any AWS SDK.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;There’s no resource to create and no API key to manage. Authenticate, call, and get results.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="using-the-web-search-api-with-agent-frameworks"&gt;Using the Web Search API with agent frameworks&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Web Search API fits naturally into popular agent frameworks. LangChain agents get it through the AgentCore web search tool in langchain-aws 1.9.3 and later:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;from langchain.agents import create_agent
from langchain.chat_models import init_chat_model
from langchain_aws.tools import create_web_search_toolkit

toolkit, tools = create_web_search_toolkit(region="us-west-2")
model = init_chat_model("bedrock_converse:us.anthropic.claude-sonnet-5", region_name="us-west-2")
agent = create_agent(model, tools=tools, system_prompt="Cite the URL of every fact you use.")&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The tool can also hold rules that the model can’t change, such as domains to exclude, a date floor, and a search budget. It returns its results as data that your code can check. For a complete example, see the &lt;a href="https://github.com/langchain-ai/langchain-aws/blob/main/samples/agents/agentcore_web_search_supply_chain_watch.ipynb" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;daily dependency watch sample&lt;/a&gt;. It researches three package ecosystems in parallel with &lt;a href="https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;LangGraph&lt;/a&gt;, and checks every finding against its sources before it reports anything.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If you prefer to wrap the call yourself, a plain LangChain tool works too:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;from langchain.tools import tool
import boto3
import json

client = boto3.client("bedrock-agentcore", region_name="us-west-2")

@tool
def web_search(query: str) -&amp;gt; str:
    """Search the web for current information."""
    response = client.invoke_web_search(
        webSearchId="web-search.v1",
        searchQuery=query,
        maxResults=5,
    )
    return json.dumps(response["results"], indent=2, default=str)

# Pass web_search to an agent, for example create_agent(model, tools=[web_search])&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Without a framework, the WebSearchClient in the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws/bedrock-agentcore-sdk-python" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Python SDK&lt;/a&gt; wraps the same call and returns results with parsed dates. langchain-aws 1.9.3 and the AgentCore Python SDK 1.26.0 add Web Search API support, so install those versions or later.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="enterprise-controls-built-in"&gt;Enterprise controls, built in&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Because the Web Search API uses standard IAM authentication, you get enterprise controls from the first call:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;IAM policies. Grant or deny &lt;code&gt;bedrock-agentcore:InvokeWebSearch&lt;/code&gt; per user, group, or role. Use &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/organizations/latest/userguide/orgs_manage_policies_scps.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;service control policies (SCPs)&lt;/a&gt; to enforce guardrails across accounts.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;AWS CloudTrail logging. CloudTrail records &lt;code&gt;InvokeWebSearch&lt;/code&gt; requests as data events. Data events aren’t logged by default, so add an event selector for the &lt;code&gt;AWS::BedrockAgentCore::WebSearch&lt;/code&gt; resource type to a trail or event data store. Events record the web search tool and &lt;code&gt;maxResults&lt;/code&gt;, not the search text or filters. For details, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/web-search-api-cloudtrail.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Log AgentCore Web Search API calls with AWS CloudTrail&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;VPC support. Use &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/vpc-interface-endpoints.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS PrivateLink&lt;/a&gt; to route API calls over your private network rather than the public internet. For setup and endpoint policies, see Private connectivity with AWS PrivateLink later in this post.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Compliance. For a list of compliance programs that cover AgentCore, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/compliance-validation.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Compliance validation for Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Searches stay in AWS. Queries are served from a web index that Amazon operates and aren’t sent to a third-party search engine.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Acceptable use. For details, see the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/web-search-api.html#web-search-api-acceptable-use" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Web Search API Acceptable Use documentation&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Quotas apply per account. For the defaults and how to request an increase, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/bedrock-agentcore-limits.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Quotas for Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;By default, each account can send up to 10 requests per second in each Region, and you can request an increase. For details, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/bedrock-agentcore-limits.html#web-search-service-limits" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Web Search Service Quotas&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="private-connectivity-with-aws-privatelink"&gt;Private connectivity with AWS PrivateLink&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Agents and pipelines that handle sensitive data often run in private subnets with no route to the internet. With &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/vpc-interface-endpoints.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS PrivateLink&lt;/a&gt;, that code can call the Web Search API through an interface VPC endpoint in your VPC. Traffic between your VPC and the API stays on the AWS network, and you don’t need an internet gateway, a NAT gateway, or a proxy to reach it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ML-22165-2.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ML-22165-2.png" alt="Diagram of a Web Search API call routed through an interface VPC endpoint over AWS PrivateLink, staying on the AWS network" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: Calling the Web Search API through AWS PrivateLink&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The numbered steps are:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Your application calls &lt;code&gt;InvokeWebSearch&lt;/code&gt; at the API’s standard Regional name. With private DNS, that name resolves to the interface endpoint in your subnet.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The endpoint policy applies, and the request reaches the API over the AWS network.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The API checks with IAM that the caller has &lt;code&gt;bedrock-agentcore:InvokeWebSearch&lt;/code&gt;, including any &lt;code&gt;aws:SourceVpce&lt;/code&gt; condition.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The API applies the request’s filters and searches the Amazon-operated web index.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The results return through the endpoint to your application.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To set it up, create an interface endpoint for the AgentCore data plane service, &lt;code&gt;com.amazonaws.&amp;lt;Region&amp;gt;.bedrock-agentcore&lt;/code&gt;, in each VPC and Region that calls the API, and turn on private DNS:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws ec2 create-vpc-endpoint \
    --region us-west-2 \
    --vpc-id vpc-0123456789abcdef0 \
    --vpc-endpoint-type Interface \
    --service-name com.amazonaws.us-west-2.bedrock-agentcore \
    --subnet-ids subnet-0123456789abcdef0 subnet-0fedcba9876543210 \
    --security-group-ids sg-0123456789abcdef0 \
    --private-dns-enabled&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With private DNS turned on, the API’s standard Regional name, &lt;code&gt;bedrock-agentcore.us-west-2.amazonaws.com&lt;/code&gt;, resolves to the endpoint’s private IP addresses inside your VPC. The Boto3 and AWS CLI examples in this post run unchanged, with no endpoint URL to set. The endpoint’s security group must allow inbound HTTPS (port 443) from the resources that call the API.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;PrivateLink also gives you controls on top of IAM:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Endpoint policies –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;A VPC endpoint policy applies to &lt;code&gt;InvokeWebSearch&lt;/code&gt; requests in addition to your IAM policies, so you decide which principals can search through the endpoint. The same endpoint also carries the other AgentCore data plane APIs, such as AgentCore runtime, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, Memory, Identity, Browser, and Code Interpreter. If workloads in the VPC call them too, add statements that allow those calls. The following policy lets only one role call the API through the endpoint: 
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
    "Effect": "Allow",
    "Principal": { "AWS": "arn:aws:iam::111122223333:role/WebSearchAppRole" },
    "Action": "bedrock-agentcore:InvokeWebSearch",
    "Resource": "arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:us-west-2:aws:tool/web-search.v1"
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
  &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Require the private path –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Add an &lt;code&gt;aws:SourceVpce&lt;/code&gt; condition to the IAM policy that grants the permission. The role can then search only through your endpoint, and the same call from outside the VPC is denied: 
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
    "Effect": "Allow",
    "Action": "bedrock-agentcore:InvokeWebSearch",
    "Resource": "arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:us-west-2:aws:tool/web-search.v1",
    "Condition": { "StringEquals": { "aws:SourceVpce": "vpce-0123456789abcdef0" } }
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
  &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Network-level control –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Security groups and subnet placement decide which instances, containers, and functions can reach the endpoint at all.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;PrivateLink works the same way in every Region where the Web Search API is available. Interface endpoints are billed at standard &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/privatelink/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS PrivateLink pricing&lt;/a&gt;. For details, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/web-search-api-privatelink.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Call AgentCore Web Search through AWS PrivateLink&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="try-it-in-the-console"&gt;Try it in the console&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can try the Web Search API in the Amazon Bedrock AgentCore console before you write any code. In the navigation pane, under &lt;strong&gt;Connections&lt;/strong&gt;, choose &lt;strong&gt;Web Search&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ml-22165-3-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ml-22165-3-1.png" alt="Web Search in the Amazon Bedrock AgentCore console" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 3: Web Search in the Amazon Bedrock AgentCore console&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Configure. In the Configure panel, set: 
  &lt;ol type="a"&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;Max results: how many results each query returns, from 1–25. The console starts at 10.&lt;/li&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;Published date: a date range in &lt;code&gt;YYYY/MM/DD&lt;/code&gt; format. Only results published inside it come back. Leave it empty to include results from any date.&lt;/li&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;Domain filtering: up to 100 domains to include and up to 100 to exclude. Choose &lt;strong&gt;View and manage domains&lt;/strong&gt; to edit the lists.&lt;/li&gt; 
  &lt;/ol&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Test. Enter a query of up to 200 characters in the Test Web Search API panel. Each query calls the Web Search API with your settings. The panel shows how many results came back and how long the call took. For each result it shows the title, a text snippet, the publication date and the source URL. Figure 4 shows a search for “How do I set up Amazon S3 lifecycle rules?” with results limited to &lt;code&gt;aws.amazon.com&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;docs.aws.amazon.com&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ml-22165-4-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ml-22165-4-1.png" alt="A test search with included domains" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 4: A test search with included domains&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1" start="3"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Integrate. When the results look right, you have two options: 
  &lt;ol type="a"&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;Generate code snippet: get code that calls the API with the same settings, ready to paste into your agent or application.&lt;/li&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;Create gateway target: add Web Search as a tool on an AgentCore Gateway, so agents can call it through MCP.&lt;/li&gt; 
  &lt;/ol&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The console signs each call with your own IAM identity, so your role needs the &lt;code&gt;bedrock-agentcore:InvokeWebSearch&lt;/code&gt; permission.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="when-to-use-the-api-compared-to-the-gateway"&gt;When to use the API compared to the gateway&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Scenario&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Recommended path&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Building agents with MCP-compatible tools&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;AgentCore Gateway&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Several tools behind one MCP endpoint, with optional &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/gateway-sessions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;MCP sessions&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;AgentCore Gateway&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;One administrator source policy for many agents and teams&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;AgentCore Gateway&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;MCP clients that need search, such as Claude with the AWS Web Search connector&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;AgentCore Gateway&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Custom orchestration&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Web Search API&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Non-agent use cases (dashboards, pipelines)&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Web Search API&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Want direct control over retries and caching&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Web Search API&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Adding web search to an existing application&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Web Search API&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Both paths search the same index. They differ in a few ways. A gateway can also authorize callers with a JSON Web Token (JWT), while the API authorizes with IAM only. On a gateway, an administrator can set domain lists on the target that apply to every request without the calling agent seeing them. With the API, each request carries its own filters. The field names differ too: the API takes &lt;code&gt;searchQuery&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;publishedAfter&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;publishedBefore&lt;/code&gt;, and the gateway’s MCP tool takes &lt;code&gt;query&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;from&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;to&lt;/code&gt;. For more guidance, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/web-search-api.html#web-search-api-choose" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Choose between the API and the Gateway connector&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="pricing"&gt;Pricing&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Web Search API meters to the same usage type as the gateway connector, so it’s billed at the Web Search rate of $7 per 1,000 queries. Because no gateway is involved, there are no gateway charges. For details, see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore pricing&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="availability"&gt;Availability&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Web Search API is available in the US East (N. Virginia), US East (Ohio), US West (Oregon), Europe (Ireland), Asia Pacific (Tokyo), and Asia Pacific (Sydney) Regions. For the current list, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/agentcore-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Supported AWS Regions&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="get-started"&gt;Get started&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Use the following resources to help you get started.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/web-search-api.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Web Search API documentation&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/grounding/web-search/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Web Search on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cli/latest/reference/bedrock-agentcore/invoke-web-search.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;InvokeWebSearch API reference&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt;

   &lt;img loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ML-22165-7.jpg" alt="Kalyan Garimella" width="100" height="133"&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt; 
  &lt;h3 class="lb-h4"&gt;Kalyan Garimella&lt;/h3&gt; 
  &lt;p style="overflow: hidden"&gt;Kalyan is a Principal Product Manager at Amazon AGI, where he leads the development and launch of web search capabilities for Amazon Bedrock AgentCore. His work focuses on solving a core limitation of modern AI agents; their inability to access real-time, factual information; by enabling agents to ground their reasoning in live web data. Kalyan lives in the Bay Area with his family.&lt;/p&gt; 
 &lt;/div&gt;

  &lt;h3 class="lb-h4"&gt;Omar Abdelwahab&lt;/h3&gt;

He holds a Ph.D.&amp;nbsp;in Computer Science and enjoys working at the intersection of AI, technology, and go-to-market strategy to help customers build innovative applications.&lt;/p&gt;

		<item>
		<title>ICYMI: What landed for AI builders in October 2026</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/icymi-what-landed-for-ai-builders-in-october-2026/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Tymofii Kryvtsun]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 11 Nov 2026 22:26:40 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Intermediate (200)]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">3f4d5141e9f40cbc6421464d50f65cb07b20edb9</guid>

					<description>A recap of the major Amazon Bedrock, Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, and Strands updates from October 2026: new models to match each task, updates that keep agents available and grounded, and clearer cost reporting for AI builders.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;At AWS, we believe AI builders should be able to choose the right model for every task, trust their agents in production, and see clearly what their AI workloads cost. That belief guides how we invest across our portfolio. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt; gives you access to leading models and the tools to build and scale AI applications securely. We extend this foundation with &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;, which provides the platform to build, connect, and optimize agents using any model and any framework. That includes &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands&lt;/a&gt;, our open source agent development toolkit. Together, they take you from first prototype to production.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;As agents move into core business workflows, the bar rises. You need the flexibility to use different models as tasks, budgets, and latency targets change. When a primary model is throttled or unreachable, your agents must keep working. They also must act within limits you set and return output other software can use as is. Their answers must stay grounded in sources you allow, and their cost must hold up at high volume.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;What we shipped in October addresses each of these needs. New models help you match each task to the right price and speed, from high-volume subagents to real-time voice. Amazon Bedrock and AgentCore updates keep agents available, hold them to the processes you define, and help you trust what they return. New pricing and cost reporting make spend more predictable. Strands adds new ways to build, contain, and test agents.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="match-the-model-to-the-task-on-amazon-bedrock"&gt;Match the model to the task on Amazon Bedrock&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Run fast, low-cost subagents.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/claude-haiku-5-5-aws/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Claude Haiku 5.5&lt;/a&gt; is built for subagents and high-volume, cost-sensitive work. According to Anthropic, it is the most capable Haiku yet and costs around 75 percent less than Claude Haiku 4.5 for most tasks. It is also the first Haiku with effort controls, so you can tune cost against intelligence for each task. In a multi-agent setup, a model such as Claude Opus 5.5 can plan the work and hand well-defined coding, tool use, and browser automation tasks to Haiku. This keeps running many agents in parallel affordable.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Get near-flagship results at a lower price.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/openai-gpt-6-1-terra-on-amazon-bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;OpenAI GPT-6.1 Terra&lt;/a&gt; is now generally available on Amazon Bedrock. On OpenAI’s Agents’ Last Exam evaluation, it performed on par with GPT-6 Astra and completed tasks more than three times faster than GPT-6 Astra and GPT-6.1 Sol. Its input and output token prices are at least 50 percent lower than those of GPT-6.1 Sol and GPT-5.6 Terra. It accepts text and images with up to 1 million input tokens, which suits drafting proposals, interpreting technical drawings, and building agents that work across business systems. For latency-sensitive work such as real-time coding assistants, GPT-6.1 Sol now also offers &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/openai-gpt-sol-ultrafast-amazon/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Ultrafast mode&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Build voice agents that reason in real time.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/amazon-nova-2.5-sonic/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Nova 2.5 Sonic&lt;/a&gt;, our latest speech-to-speech model, improves reasoning, instruction following, and tool-calling accuracy, with lower latency at the same price as Amazon Nova 2 Sonic. A customer service agent can now look up an order, check return eligibility, and start a return in a single conversation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Pick specialized models for coding and video.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/amazon-bedrock-glm-5-3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;GLM 5.3 by Z.ai&lt;/a&gt; is an open-weight model for agentic coding and long-horizon software engineering, with a 1-million-token context window and prompt caching, available to eligible enterprise customers. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/twelve-labs-pegasus-1-5-aws/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;TwelveLabs Pegasus 1.5&lt;/a&gt; turns entire videos into structured, time-coded metadata. It reads visuals, audio, and on-screen text in a single pass, with no separate ingestion pipeline.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Use more models in AWS GovCloud (US).&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/claude-haiku-5-5-aws-govcloud/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Claude Haiku 5.5&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/bedrock-openai-runtime-govcloud/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;OpenAI GPT-5.6 models with cross-Region inference&lt;/a&gt; are now available in AWS GovCloud (US). &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/openai-daybreak-red-govcloud-east/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Daybreak Red from OpenAI&lt;/a&gt;, a model for authorized security work, is available to eligible customers in AWS GovCloud (US-East). Regulated teams get more models while maintaining US data sovereignty.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="build-and-run-agents-you-can-rely-on"&gt;Build and run agents you can rely on&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Build and operate agents from your terminal.&lt;/strong&gt; AgentCore CLI, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, is now &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/amazon-bedrock-agentcore-cli-version-1-0-available/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;generally available in version 1.0&lt;/a&gt;. We rebuilt it with faster startup, a consistent command structure, and support for more AgentCore resources. You can work with harnesses, runtimes, memory, identity, gateways, and payments directly or through a project. Existing projects move to version 1 with one command. The result is one tool from local development to day-to-day operations.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Keep agents available when a model is throttled.&lt;/strong&gt; AgentCore Gateway, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, now &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/agentcore-gateway-invokemodel-fallback/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;supports the Amazon Bedrock InvokeModel APIs and model fallback&lt;/a&gt;. Your existing Amazon Bedrock SDK code can point at the gateway without per-provider code changes. When the primary model is throttled or unreachable, the gateway routes the request to the next model or provider in a chain you define. A circuit breaker skips destinations that are failing. The gateway can also reach private targets in your virtual private cloud (VPC) using &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/agentcore-gateway-private-tls-vpc/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;certificates from your own private certificate authority&lt;/a&gt;. Your agents stay available without retry logic in every client.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Turn your business workflows into agent tools.&lt;/strong&gt; You can now use &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/step-functions-workflows-ai-agent-tools/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Step Functions workflows&lt;/a&gt;, including your existing Standard and Express workflows, as tools through AgentCore Gateway. Your business logic stays in a workflow you already run and trust, so you don’t rebuild it inside the agent or rely on a prompt to follow it. The agent decides when to run a workflow, and Step Functions runs each step exactly as you defined it. For example, a support agent can start your refund workflow, and every refund still goes through the same eligibility check, approval, and credit steps.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Get JSON your systems can use as is.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/claude-structured-outputs-bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Structured outputs&lt;/a&gt; are now available for all Claude models on Amazon Bedrock. You receive schema-compliant JSON without custom parsing or retries. You can extract every required field from an earnings report, or route support tickets with entities that always match your data model.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Keep answers grounded in sources you trust.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/bedrock-web-search-domain-user-location/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Web Search on Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt; now supports domain filtering and user location for requests made with the OpenAI Responses API. You can allow up to 100 domains, block up to 100 domains, and return results relevant to a geography. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/bedrock-guardrails-grounding-standard/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Guardrails&lt;/a&gt; then checks that answers stay true to their sources. Its new Standard tier for contextual grounding checks costs the same as the Classic tier, covers conversational and reasoning responses, and supports more languages. With both, you can control where answers come from and catch hallucinations in more of your applications.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="predict-and-lower-the-cost-of-running-agents"&gt;Predict and lower the cost of running agents&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Pay for agent memory based on what you use.&lt;/strong&gt; AgentCore memory, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, now &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/agentcore-memory-consumption-based-pricing" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;prices short-term memory by the data your agents ingest, retrieve, and store&lt;/a&gt;, instead of a flat rate per event. Most workloads see lower costs, and small-payload use cases see the largest reductions. You can also set a &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/agentcore-memory-long-term-memory-ttl" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;time-to-live on long-term memory records&lt;/a&gt; to meet retention requirements and control storage costs.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;See what drives your Amazon Bedrock spend.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/aws-bedrock-attributes-in-cost-explorer/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Cost Explorer, AWS Budgets, and AWS Cost Management Dashboards&lt;/a&gt; now break down Amazon Bedrock cost by model, model provider, inference type, and feature, at no additional charge. Finance and platform teams can find their most expensive models or set a budget alert for a single model, in the billing tools they already use. To lower input costs, &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/bedrock-prompt-optimization-shortening/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Advanced Prompt Optimization&lt;/a&gt; can now also shorten your prompts, returning a shorter version only when it scores at least as high on your evaluation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="build-voice-local-and-well-tested-agents-with-strands"&gt;Build voice, local, and well-tested agents with Strands&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Ship voice agents in a few lines of code.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/blog/bidi-agents-now-ga/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Bidi Agents&lt;/a&gt;, for building two-way, speech-to-speech voice agents, are now generally available, with direct support for Amazon Nova Sonic, OpenAI, and Gemini models. Bidi Agents reconnect to the model in the background, so long calls keep going past a provider’s session limit. A voice agent can also hand longer work, such as research, to another agent and explain the results aloud.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Let local agents work within your limits.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/blog/strands-box-the-big-picture/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Box&lt;/a&gt;, now in developer preview, is an open source local sandbox for AI agents. It combines operating-system isolation with semantic policy. You set policy for each tool, and Box enforces it deterministically, whether the agent calls tools directly, through the Model Context Protocol (MCP), or by generating code. Box works with any model and any agent framework, so an agent can operate in your project without reaching files and services you didn’t allow.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Find where your agent breaks before customers do.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/blog/red-team-your-agent-strands-evals/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Red teaming in Strands Evals&lt;/a&gt; runs adversarial conversations, such as prompt injections and jailbreak attempts, against your agent and shows the messages and tool calls where it failed. Strands Evals can now also &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/blog/structured-output-similarity-strands-evals/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;score structured output field by field&lt;/a&gt;, giving partial credit when an agent gets most fields right. Use them to fix weak spots before an agent reaches production. To get started, see the &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/docs/user-guide/evals-sdk/quickstart/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Evals documentation&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cut the cost of routine agent decisions.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/blog/strands-decider-gemma-4-models/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Decider&lt;/a&gt;, the small, open source decision model we featured in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/icymi-what-landed-for-ai-builders-in-september-2026/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;last month’s recap&lt;/a&gt;, now comes in four sizes built on Gemma 4. Instead of generating text, it picks between options you define, such as which tool to call or where to route a request. When an Amazon team &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/blog/amazon-saves-millions-replacing-sonnet-with-strands-decider/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;switched ticket classification in one of its largest internal ticketing systems to Decider&lt;/a&gt;, estimated cost per ticket dropped by about 98 percent. Average response time dropped by 78 percent.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="get-started"&gt;Get started&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Explore &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;, deploy agents with the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/agentcore-get-started-cli.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore CLI&lt;/a&gt;, or build your first agent with the &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Harness SDK&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Interested in learning how Amazon Bedrock can support your team? &lt;a href="https://pages.awscloud.com/Amazon-Bedrock-Contact-Us.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Connect with us&lt;/a&gt; to start the conversation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;
